\mathcal{X}\cap\mathrm{Z}(\mathscr{P}_{d}^{n})=\{\mathbf{I}\}
. Thus
\mathcal{X}=\{\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}:\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}\}
for some set
\mathscr{X}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
and
s:\mathscr{X}\to\mathbb{Z}_{d}
. That
\mathscr{X}
is a submodule follows from
\mathcal{X}
being a group. It is isotropic because, given
\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}},\omega^{s(\mathbf{y})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}\in\mathcal{X}
, then
(\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}})^{\dagger}(\omega^{s(\mathbf{y})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}})^{\dagger}(\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}})(\omega^{s(\mathbf{y})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}})=\omega^{[\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}]}\cdot\mathbf{I}
is an element of
\mathcal{X}
, which implies that
[\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}]=0
since
\mathcal{X}\cap\mathrm{Z}(\mathscr{P}_{d}^{n})=\{\mathbf{I}\}
. Now notice that
(\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}})(\omega^{s(\mathbf{y})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}})=\tau^{[\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}]}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+s(\mathbf{y})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}}
, where
[\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}]=0~(\operatorname{mod}d)
due to commutativity, therefore, by the uniqueness of phase of each Weyl operator,
s(\mathbf{x})+s(\mathbf{y})=s(\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y})
if
d
is odd, and
\omega^{s(\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y})}=\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+s(\mathbf{y})}\tau^{[\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}]}
is
d
is even. Moreover,
s(\mathbf{0})=0
again due to
\mathcal{X}\cap\mathrm{Z}(\mathscr{P}_{d}^{n})=\{\mathbf{I}\}
. Therefore, for
d
odd,
\omega^{s(\cdot)}
is a character and we can write
s(\mathbf{x})=[\mathbf{a},\mathbf{x}]
for some
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
. Finally, we prove that
\mathscr{X}
is Lagrangian if
\mathcal{X}
is maximal. Assume by contradiction that exists
\mathbf{y}\in\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}
such that
\mathbf{y}\notin\mathscr{X}
. This would mean that
\omega^{s(\mathbf{y})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}\notin\mathcal{X}
while
\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}
commutes with all elements of
\mathcal{X}
, a contradiction since
\mathcal{X}
is maximal. Therefore
\mathbf{y}\in\mathscr{X}
and
\mathscr{X}
is Lagrangian. As a consequence,
|\mathscr{X}|=d^{n}
.
∎

Corollary 19
.

Any stabiliser group

\mathcal{S}
of
\mathscr{P}_{d}^{n}
is commutative (abelian) and has size
|\mathcal{S}|=d^{n}
.

Given a generating set
\{(\mathbf{v}_{i},\mathbf{w}_{i})\}_{i\in[\ell]}
for
\mathscr{M}
, let the matrices
\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n\times\ell}
with columns
\mathbf{v}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{v}_{\ell}
and
\mathbf{w}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{w}_{\ell}
, respectively. Then
\mathscr{M}={\operatorname{col}}\big(\big[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{V}\\
\mathbf{W}\end{smallmatrix}\big]\big)
. Note that
\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}
since
\mathscr{M}
is isotropic.

Together with the concept of stabiliser group is the concept of stabiliser state, which is the joint
+1
-eigenstate of all elements of a stabiliser group.

Definition 20

(Partial stabiliser state)
.

A non-zero state
|\psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
is
stabilised
by
\mathcal{P}\in\mathscr{P}^{n}_{d}
if
\mathcal{P}|\psi\rangle=|\psi\rangle
. A non-zero state
|\Psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
is a
partial stabiliser state
of a partial stabiliser group
\mathcal{X}
if
\mathcal{P}|\Psi\rangle=|\Psi\rangle
for all
\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{X}
. Let
\mathcal{X}_{d}^{n}(K)
denote the set of all partial stabiliser states in
(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
of partial stabiliser groups
\mathcal{X}
of size at least
K
.

Definition 21

(Stabiliser state)
.

A non-zero state
|\Psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
is a
stabiliser state
of a stabiliser group
\mathcal{S}
if
\mathcal{P}|\Psi\rangle=|\Psi\rangle
for all
\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{S}
. Let
\mathcal{S}_{d}^{n}
be the set of all stabiliser states in
(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
. Clearly
\mathcal{S}_{d}^{n}=\mathcal{X}_{d}^{n}(d^{n})
.

Consider the stabiliser group
\mathcal{S}=\{\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}:\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M}\}
and let
V_{\mathcal{S}}\triangleq\{|\Psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}:\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\Psi\rangle=|\Psi\rangle,\forall\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M}\}
be the set of stabiliser states of
\mathcal{S}
. The next lemma constructs an projector onto
V_{\mathcal{S}}
and shows that any stabiliser group has a unique stabiliser state.

Lemma 22
.

Let
\mathcal{X}=\{\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}:\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}\}
be a partial stabiliser group and let
V_{\mathcal{X}}\triangleq\{|\Psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}:\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\Psi\rangle=|\Psi\rangle,\forall\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}\}
. Then the projector onto
V_{\mathcal{X}}
is

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathcal{X}}=\frac{1}{|\mathscr{X}|}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}.

Moreover,
\operatorname{dim}(V_{\mathcal{X}})=d^{n}/|\mathscr{X}|
. It then follows that any stabiliser group
\mathcal{S}
has a unique stabiliser state, i.e.,
\operatorname{dim}(V_{\mathcal{S}})=1
.

Proof.

Consider the operators
\Pi_{\mathbf{a}}=|\mathscr{X}|^{-1}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+[\mathbf{a},\mathbf{x}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}
for
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
. It is easy to see that
\Pi_{\mathbf{a}}
is Hermitian by the change of index
\mathbf{x}\mapsto-\mathbf{x}
since
s(-\mathbf{x})=-s(\mathbf{x})
. It is also idempotent,

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbf{a}}^{2}=\frac{1}{|\mathscr{X}|^{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+s(\mathbf{y})+[\mathbf{a},\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}=\frac{1}{|\mathscr{X}|^{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y})+[\mathbf{a},\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}}=\Pi_{\mathbf{a}},

where we used that
\omega^{s(\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y})}=\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+s(\mathbf{y})}\tau^{[\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}]}
(which is valid for both odd and even
d
). Therefore,
\Pi_{\mathbf{a}}
(and so
\Pi_{\mathcal{X}}
) is a projector. Moreover,
\Pi_{\mathcal{X}}|\Psi\rangle=|\Psi\rangle
for any
|\Psi\rangle\in V_{\mathcal{X}}
. On the other hand, given
\Pi_{\mathcal{X}}|\psi\rangle
for any
|\psi\rangle
,
\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}(\Pi_{\mathcal{X}}|\psi\rangle)=\Pi_{\mathcal{X}}|\psi\rangle
for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}
, and thus
\Pi_{\mathcal{X}}|\psi\rangle\in V_{\mathcal{X}}
. This proves that
\Pi_{\mathcal{X}}
is the projector onto
V_{\mathcal{X}}
.

Due to the Pontryagin duality theorem (see
[
Rud90
, Theorem 1.7.2]
) and to
\mathscr{X}
being a finite group, there are
|\mathscr{X}|
characters of
\mathscr{X}
(since the set of characters of
\mathscr{X}
is isomorphic to
\mathscr{X}
). Each character gives rise to a distinct projector
\Pi_{C}=|\mathscr{X}|^{-1}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+[\mathbf{a}_{C},\mathbf{x}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}
where
C\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}/\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}
is a coset and
\mathbf{a}_{C}
is any element from
C
. Two different projectors are orthogonal since they belong to different eigenvalues of at least one Weyl operator or, alternatively,

\displaystyle\Pi_{C}\Pi_{C^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{|\mathscr{X}|^{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+s(\mathbf{y})+[\mathbf{a}_{C},\mathbf{x}]+[\mathbf{a}_{C^{\prime}},\mathbf{y}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}=\frac{1}{|\mathscr{X}|^{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{z})+[\mathbf{a}_{C},\mathbf{x}]+[\mathbf{a}_{C^{\prime}},\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{x}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}}=0,

using that
\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+s(\mathbf{y})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}=\omega^{s(\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}}
and
\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{[\mathbf{a}_{C}-\mathbf{a}_{C^{\prime}},\mathbf{x}]}=0
since
\mathbf{a}_{C}-\mathbf{a}_{C^{\prime}}\neq\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}
(
Lemma

9
). This means that the rank of each projector must be a
|\mathscr{X}|
-fraction of the Hilbert space dimension
d^{n}
, i.e.,
d^{n}/|\mathscr{X}|
. This implies that
\operatorname{dim}(V_{\mathcal{X}})=\operatorname{rank}(\Pi_{C})=d^{n}/|\mathscr{X}|
.
∎

Following
[
Gro06
,
GNW21
]
, we can denote the unique stabiliser state
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
of a stabiliser group
\mathcal{S}=\{\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}:\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M}\}
as
|\mathscr{M},s\rangle
, where
\mathscr{M}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
and
s:\mathscr{M}\to\mathbb{Z}_{d}
are the Lagrangian submodule and phase function defining
\mathcal{S}
, respectively. We note that we can obtain another function from
s
by replacing it with
s^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})=s(\mathbf{x})+[\mathbf{z},\mathbf{x}]
for some
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
, in which case
|\mathscr{M},s^{\prime}\rangle=\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}}|\mathscr{M},s\rangle
. Even more precisely, any two strings
\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\prime}
from a coset
C\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}/\mathscr{M}
yields the same stabiliser state
|\mathscr{M},s+[\mathbf{z},\cdot]\rangle
since then
\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{z}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{M}
. Given
\mathscr{M}
and
s
, we can write the stabiliser state
|\mathscr{M},s+[\mathbf{z},\cdot]\rangle
as
|\mathscr{M},s,C\rangle
where
\mathbf{z}\in C
and
C\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}/\mathscr{M}
. Conversely, given a Lagrangian subspace
\mathscr{M}
, any state that is a simultaneous eigenvector of
\{\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\}_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M}}
is a stabiliser state of some stabiliser group. The eigenvectors of
\{\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\}_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M}}
thus determine a stabiliser basis
\{|\mathscr{M},s\rangle\}_{s}=\{|\mathscr{M},s,C\rangle\}_{C\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}/\mathscr{M}}
.

Consider a stabiliser group
\mathcal{S}=\{\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}:\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M}\}
. According to
Lemma

22
, the operator
d^{-n}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}
is the orthogonal projection onto the set
V_{\mathcal{S}}
spanned by the stabiliser state
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
of
\mathcal{S}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{S}\rangle\langle\mathcal{S}|=\frac{1}{d^{n}}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}

(1)

and the characteristic function and probability of
|\mathcal{S}\rangle\langle\mathcal{S}|
are

\displaystyle c_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{x})=\begin{cases}d^{-n/2}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}&\text{if}~\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M},\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}\qquad\qquad\text{and}\qquad\qquad p_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{x})=\begin{cases}d^{-n}&\text{if}~\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M},\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(2)

Thus
p_{\mathcal{S}}
is the uniform distribution on the subspace
\mathscr{M}
, a fact which will be of paramount importance when studying Bell (difference) sampling in the next section. While we have obtained an expression for
|\mathcal{S}\rangle\langle\mathcal{S}|
, in the following lemma, we obtain an expression for
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
. We note that a similar form for stabiliser states was given in
[
HDDM05
, Theorem 1]
.

Lemma 23
.

Let
\mathcal{S}=\langle\{\omega^{s_{i}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{v}_{i},\mathbf{w}_{i}}\}_{i\in[\ell]}\rangle
be a stabiliser group.
Let
\mathbf{s}=(s_{1},\dots,s_{\ell})\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell}
and let
\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n\times\ell}
be the matrices with column vectors
\mathbf{v}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{v}_{\ell}
and
\mathbf{w}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{w}_{\ell}
, respectively. Then the unique stabiliser state of
\mathcal{S}
is

\displaystyle|\mathcal{S}\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d^{\ell}|\operatorname{null}(\mathbf{W})|}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell}}\omega^{\mathbf{q}^{\top}(\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{u})}\cdot\tau^{\mathbf{q}^{\top}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{u}\rangle

for any
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n}
such that
\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{u}\in\operatorname{row}(\mathbf{W})
(the multiplication in
\mathbf{q}^{\top}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}
is over
\mathbb{Z}_{D}
).

Proof.

First note that the unormalised state
|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{u}}\rangle\triangleq|\mathcal{S}\rangle\langle\mathcal{S}|\mathbf{u}\rangle
, for any
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n}
, is either the stabiliser state
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
of
\mathcal{S}
or the zero state. Then

\displaystyle|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d^{n}}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\mathbf{u}\rangle

(by
Equation

1
)

\displaystyle~\propto\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell}}\omega^{s(q_{1}\mathbf{x}_{1}+\cdots+q_{\ell}\mathbf{x}_{\ell})}\mathcal{W}_{q_{1}\mathbf{x}_{1}+\cdots+q_{\ell}\mathbf{x}_{\ell}}|\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell}}\omega^{\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}q_{i}s(\mathbf{x}_{i})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{q},\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}}|\mathbf{u}\rangle

(since
[\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{x}_{j}]=0
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell}}\omega^{\mathbf{q}^{\top}(\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{u})}\cdot\tau^{\mathbf{q}^{\top}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{u}\rangle.

To analyse the case when
|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{u}}\rangle
is the stabiliser state, we compute
|\langle\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{u}}|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{u}}\rangle|
as follows:

\displaystyle|\langle\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{u}}|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{u}}\rangle|

\displaystyle~\propto\left|\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell}}\omega^{(\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{u})^{\top}(\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{q}^{\prime})}\cdot\tau^{\mathbf{q}^{\top}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{q}^{\prime\top}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}\langle\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}|\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{null}(\mathbf{W})}\omega^{(\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{u})^{\top}\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}\cdot\tau^{\mathbf{q}^{\top}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}-(\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{q}^{\prime})^{\top}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{W}(\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{q}^{\prime})}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{null}(\mathbf{W})}\omega^{(\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{u})^{\top}\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}\right|

(by
\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}
and
\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}^{\prime}=\mathbf{0}
)

\displaystyle=d^{\ell}\left|\sum_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{null}(\mathbf{W})}\omega^{(\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{u})^{\top}\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}\right|.

According to
Lemmas

5
and
9
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{null}(\mathbf{W})}\omega^{(\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{u})^{\top}\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}|\operatorname{null}(\mathbf{W})|&\text{if}~\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{u}\in\operatorname{null}(\mathbf{W})^{\perp}=\operatorname{row}(\mathbf{W}),\\
0&\text{if}~\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{u}\notin\operatorname{null}(\mathbf{W})^{\perp}=\operatorname{row}(\mathbf{W}).\end{cases}

Thus
|\langle\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{u}}|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{u}}\rangle|~\propto~d^{\ell}|\operatorname{null}(\mathbf{W})|
if
\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{u}\in\operatorname{row}(\mathbf{W})
, and
|\langle\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{u}}|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{u}}\rangle|=0
otherwise. Finally, it is clear that there always is
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n}
such that
\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{u}\in\operatorname{row}(\mathbf{W})
, otherwise
|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{u}}\rangle=0
for all
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n}
and
|\mathcal{S}\rangle\langle\mathcal{S}|
would not be an orthogonal projection.
∎

2.6
Stabiliser size

Sometimes we are interested in the Pauli operators that stabilise a given pure state
|\psi\rangle
up to a phase. This is captured by the
unsigned stabiliser group
of
|\psi\rangle
.

Definition 24

(Unsigned stabiliser group and stabiliser size)
.

Given
|\psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
, its
unsigned stabiliser group
is
\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)\triangleq\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}:|\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\psi\rangle|=1\}
and its
stabiliser size
is
|\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)|
.

Remark 25
.

Note that for all
|\psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
,
\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)
is a partial stabiliser group. On the other hand, for any partial stabiliser group
\mathcal{X}
, there always is
|\psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
such that
\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)=\mathcal{X}
.

Lemma 26
.

For any non-zero
|\psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
,
\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)
is an isotropic submodule.

Proof.

Suppose there are
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)
such that
[\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}]\neq 0~(\operatorname{mod}d)
. On the one hand,
\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}|\psi\rangle=\omega^{[\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\psi\rangle=\omega^{[\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}]+s(\mathbf{x})+s(\mathbf{y})}|\psi\rangle
, while on the other hand,
\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}|\psi\rangle=\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+s(\mathbf{y})}|\psi\rangle
, which is a contradiction. Moreover, given
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)
, then
\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}}|\psi\rangle=\tau^{[\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}|\psi\rangle=\tau^{[\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}]}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+s(\mathbf{y})}|\psi\rangle
, but since
[\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}]=0~(\operatorname{mod}d)
, either
\tau^{[\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}]}=1
or
\tau^{[\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}]}=\omega^{d/2}
, the latter for
d
even only. Thus
\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}\in\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)
and
\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)
is a submodule.
∎

The stabiliser size is invariant under Clifford transformations as a corollary of the next fact.

Definition 27

(Symplectic matrix)
.

A matrix
\Gamma\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n\times 2n}
is called
symplectic
if it preserves the symplectic product, i.e.,
[\Gamma\mathbf{x},\Gamma\mathbf{y}]=[\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}]
for all
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
. Equivalently,
\Gamma
satisfies
\Gamma^{\top}\Omega\Gamma=\Omega
, where
\Omega\triangleq\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{n}&\mathbf{I}_{n}\\
-\mathbf{I}_{n}&\mathbf{0}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)
. Let
\operatorname{Sp}(2n,d)
be the set of symplectic matrices over
\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
.

Fact 28

(
[
DB13
, Theorem 5]
)
.

Any Clifford operator
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{d}^{n}
is of the form
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{a}}\mu(\Gamma)
for some
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
and a unitary operator
\mu(\Gamma)\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
such that
\mu(\Gamma)\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\mu(\Gamma)^{\dagger}=\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma\mathbf{x}}
for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
, where
\Gamma\in\operatorname{Sp}(2n,d)
.

Corollary 29
.

For all
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{d}^{n}
and
|\psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
,
|\operatorname{Weyl}(\mathcal{C}|\psi\rangle)|=|\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)|
.

Proof.

Write
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{a}}\mu(\Gamma)
for some
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
and
\Gamma\in\operatorname{Sp}(2n,d)
. The result follows because
|\operatorname{Weyl}(\mathcal{C}|\psi\rangle)|=|\operatorname{Weyl}(\mu(\Gamma)|\psi\rangle)|
and
\Gamma
is invertible (
\Gamma^{-1}=\Omega^{-1}\Gamma^{\top}\Omega
), so that
\operatorname{Weyl}(\mu(\Gamma)|\psi\rangle)=\{\Gamma\mathbf{x}:\mathbf{x}\in\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)\}
and
\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)=\{\Gamma^{-1}\mathbf{x}:\mathbf{x}\in\operatorname{Weyl}(\mu(\Gamma)|\psi\rangle)\}
. Indeed, it is not hard to see that
\{\Gamma\mathbf{x}:\mathbf{x}\in\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)\}\subseteq\operatorname{Weyl}(\mu(\Gamma)|\psi\rangle)
and
\{\Gamma^{-1}\mathbf{x}:\mathbf{x}\in\operatorname{Weyl}(\mu(\Gamma)|\psi\rangle)\}\subseteq\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)
. Assume by contradiction that there is
\mathbf{y}\in\operatorname{Weyl}(\mu(\Gamma)|\psi\rangle)
such that
\mathbf{y}\notin\{\Gamma\mathbf{x}:\mathbf{x}\in\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)\}
. Then
\Gamma^{-1}\mathbf{y}\in\{\Gamma^{-1}\mathbf{x}:\mathbf{x}\in\operatorname{Weyl}(\mu(\Gamma)|\psi\rangle)\}
but
\Gamma^{-1}\mathbf{y}\notin\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)
, a contradiction. Similarly for
\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)=\{\Gamma^{-1}\mathbf{x}:\mathbf{x}\in\operatorname{Weyl}(\mu(\Gamma)|\psi\rangle)\}
.
∎

The next lemma covers another important property of unsigned stabiliser groups: the stabiliser size is at most
d^{n}
and is maximised for stabiliser states.

Lemma 30
.

For any
|\psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
,
|\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)|\leq d^{n}
, with equality if and only if
|\psi\rangle
is a stabiliser state.

Proof.

A simple consequence of
\operatorname{Weyl}(|\psi\rangle)
being isotropic and any isotropic set of elements having cardinality at most
d^{n}
, with equality if and only if the set is Lagrangian.
∎

2.7
Stabiliser fidelity

Alongside its stabiliser size, another important measure of the “stabiliser complexity” of a quantum state is its
stabiliser fidelity

[
BBC+19
]
. Here we propose a slight generalisation called “
K
-sized stabiliser fidelity”. Recall the definitions of
\mathcal{X}_{d}^{n}(K)
and
\mathcal{S}_{d}^{n}
from
Definitions

20
and
21
.

Definition 31

((
K
-sized) Stabiliser fidelity)
.

Given an
n
-qudit quantum state
|\psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
, its
K
-sized stabiliser fidelity
is
F_{K}(|\psi\rangle)\triangleq\max_{|\Psi\rangle\in\mathcal{X}_{d}^{n}(K)}|\langle\Psi|\psi\rangle|^{2}
, while its
stabiliser fidelity
is
F_{\mathcal{S}}(|\psi\rangle)\triangleq\max_{|\mathcal{S}\rangle\in\mathcal{S}_{d}^{n}}|\langle\mathcal{S}|\psi\rangle|^{2}
.

It is possible to relate the
K
-sized stabiliser fidelity
F_{K}(|\psi\rangle)
of a quantum state
|\psi\rangle
to its characteristic distribution
p_{\psi}
as shown next.

Lemma 32
.

Given
|\psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
, let
\mathscr{X}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
be the isotropic submodule characterising the partial stabiliser state that maximises
F_{K}(|\psi\rangle)
. Then

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})\leq F_{K}(|\psi\rangle)\leq\sqrt{\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})}.

Proof.

Let
|\Psi\rangle=\arg\max_{|\phi\rangle\in\mathcal{X}_{d}^{n}(K)}|\langle\phi|\psi\rangle|^{2}
and
\mathcal{X}=\{\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}:\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}\}
be its partial stabiliser group. Let
\Pi_{\mathcal{X}}=|\mathscr{X}|^{-1}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}
be the projection onto the set of partial stabiliser states of
\mathcal{X}
, i.e.,
V_{\mathcal{X}}=\{|\Psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}:\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\Psi\rangle=|\Psi\rangle,\forall\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}\}
. For the upper bound,

\displaystyle F_{K}(|\psi\rangle)

\displaystyle=\langle\psi|\Pi_{\mathcal{X}}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\mathscr{X}|}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\psi\rangle

(by
Lemma

22
)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{|\mathscr{X}|}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}|\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\psi\rangle|^{2}}

(by Cauchy-Schwarz)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{d^{n}}{|\mathscr{X}|}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})}

(definition of
p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})
)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})}.

(by
Lemma

16
)

For the lower bound, define the projections
\Pi_{C}=|\mathscr{X}|^{-1}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+[\mathbf{b},\mathbf{x}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}
from the proof of
Lemma

22
, where
C\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}/\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}
is a coset and
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
is any element from
C
. Then

\displaystyle F_{K}(|\psi\rangle)

\displaystyle=\max_{C\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}/\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}\langle\psi|\Pi_{C}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{C\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}/\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}\langle\psi|\Pi_{C}|\psi\rangle^{2}

(by
\sum_{C\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}/\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}\Pi_{C}=\mathbf{I}
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\mathscr{X}|^{2}}\sum_{C\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}/\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}\sum_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+s(\mathbf{y})+[\mathbf{b}_{C},\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}]}\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}|\psi\rangle,

(by
Lemma

22
)

where
\mathbf{b}_{C}
is any element of the coset
C
. By taking the average over all elements
\mathbf{b}\in C
, then

\displaystyle F_{K}(|\psi\rangle)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{|\mathscr{X}|^{2}|\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}|}\sum_{C\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}/\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}\sum_{\mathbf{b}\in C}\sum_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+s(\mathbf{y})+[\mathbf{b},\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}]}\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{d^{-2n}}{|\mathscr{X}|}\sum_{\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}\sum_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y})+[\mathbf{b},\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}]}\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}|\psi\rangle

(
\omega^{s(\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y})}=\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+s(\mathbf{y})}
for
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathscr{X}
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\mathscr{X}|}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{-\mathbf{x}}|\psi\rangle

(by
Lemma

9
and
s(\mathbf{0})=0
)

\displaystyle=\frac{d^{n}}{|\mathscr{X}|}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})

(definition of
p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x}).

(by
Lemma

16
)   ∎

Corollary 33
.

Given a quantum state
|\psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
, let
|\mathscr{M},s\rangle=\argmax_{|\mathcal{S}\rangle\in\mathcal{S}_{d}^{n}}|\langle\mathcal{S}|\psi\rangle|^{2}
be the stabiliser state that maximises the stabiliser fidelity
F_{\mathcal{S}}(|\psi\rangle)
. Then

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})\leq F_{\mathcal{S}}(|\psi\rangle)\leq\sqrt{\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})}.

We also prove that given an isotropic submodule
\mathscr{X}
with large enough
p_{\psi}
-mass, it is possible to find a state
|\phi\rangle
with large fidelity with
|\psi\rangle
such that
\mathscr{X}\subseteq\operatorname{Weyl}(|\phi\rangle)
.

Lemma 34
.

Let
\mathscr{X}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
be an isotropic submodule with size
d^{n}/K
and such that

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})\geq\frac{1-\varepsilon}{K}\qquad\text{for}~\varepsilon\in[0,1].

Then there is
|\phi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
with
\mathscr{X}\subseteq\operatorname{Weyl}(|\phi\rangle)
such that the fidelity between
|\psi\rangle
and
|\phi\rangle
is at least
1-\varepsilon
, i.e.,
|\langle\phi|\psi\rangle|^{2}\geq 1-\varepsilon
.

Proof.

For any suitable phase function
s:\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}\to\mathbb{Z}_{d}
, consider the partial stabiliser group
\mathcal{X}=\{\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}:\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}\}
. Define the projections
\Pi_{C}=|\mathscr{X}|^{-1}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+[\mathbf{b},\mathbf{x}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}
from the proof of
Lemma

22
, where
C\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}/\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}
is a coset and
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
is any element from
C
. Let
V_{\mathcal{X}}(C)\triangleq\{|\Psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}:\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})+[\mathbf{b},\mathbf{x}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\Psi\rangle=|\Psi\rangle,\forall\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}\}
for any
\mathbf{b}\in C
and let
|\phi\rangle
be the state that maximises
\max_{C\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}/\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}\max_{|\Psi\rangle\in V_{\mathcal{X}}(C)}|\langle\Psi|\psi\rangle|^{2}
. Clearly
\mathscr{X}\subseteq\operatorname{Weyl}(|\phi\rangle)
. By following the exact same steps as the proof
Lemma

32
, one can show that
\max_{C\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}/\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}\max_{|\Psi\rangle\in V_{\mathcal{X}}(C)}|\langle\Psi|\psi\rangle|^{2}=\max_{C\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}/\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}\langle\psi|\Pi_{C}|\psi\rangle\geq\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})
. Therefore, and also using
Lemma

16
,

\displaystyle|\langle\phi|\psi\rangle|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})=\frac{d^{n}}{|\mathscr{X}|}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})=K\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})\geq 1-\varepsilon.\qed

We now bound the value of
p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})
in terms of
F_{\mathcal{S}}(|\psi\rangle)
.

Lemma 35
.

Let
|\psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
and

|\mathscr{M},s\rangle=\arg\max_{|\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathcal{S}_{d}^{n}}|\langle\mathcal{S}^{\prime}|\psi\rangle|^{2}
such that
F_{\mathcal{S}}(|\psi\rangle)\geq\frac{1}{2}
. If
\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M}
, then
d^{n}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})\geq(2F_{\mathcal{S}}(|\psi\rangle)-1)^{2}
.

Proof.

Let
\theta\triangleq F_{\mathcal{S}}(|\psi\rangle)
for simplicity and write
|\psi\rangle=\sqrt{\theta}|\mathcal{S}\rangle+\sqrt{1-\theta}|\mathcal{S}^{\perp}\rangle
, where
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
is the stabiliser state that maximises
F_{\mathcal{S}}(|\psi\rangle)
and
|\mathcal{S}^{\perp}\rangle
is the part orthogonal to
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
. For
\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{M}
,

\displaystyle\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\psi|(\sqrt{\theta}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\mathcal{S}\rangle+\sqrt{1-\theta}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\mathcal{S}^{\perp}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\theta}\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}\langle\psi|\mathcal{S}\rangle+\sqrt{1-\theta}\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\mathcal{S}^{\perp}\rangle

(
\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\mathcal{S}\rangle=\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}|\mathcal{S}\rangle
)

\displaystyle=\theta\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}+\sqrt{1-\theta}\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\mathcal{S}^{\perp}\rangle

\displaystyle=\theta\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}+\sqrt{1-\theta}(\sqrt{\theta}\langle\mathcal{S}|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\mathcal{S}^{\perp}\rangle+\sqrt{1-\theta}\langle\mathcal{S}^{\perp}|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\mathcal{S}^{\perp}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\theta\omega^{s(\mathbf{x})}+(1-\theta)\langle\mathcal{S}^{\perp}|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\mathcal{S}^{\perp}\rangle.

(
\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}^{\dagger}|\mathcal{S}\rangle=\omega^{s(-\mathbf{x})}|\mathcal{S}\rangle
and
\langle\mathcal{S}|\mathcal{S}^{\perp}\rangle=0
)

Then

\displaystyle|\langle\psi|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\psi\rangle|^{2}

\displaystyle=\theta^{2}+(1-\theta)^{2}|\langle\mathcal{S}^{\perp}|\omega^{-s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\mathcal{S}^{\perp}\rangle|^{2}+2\theta(1-\theta)\operatorname{Re}[\langle\mathcal{S}|\omega^{-s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}^{\dagger}|\mathcal{S}^{\perp}\rangle]

\displaystyle\geq\theta^{2}+(1-\theta)^{2}\operatorname{Re}[\langle\mathcal{S}|\omega^{-s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}^{\dagger}|\mathcal{S}^{\perp}\rangle]^{2}+2\theta(1-\theta)\operatorname{Re}[\langle\mathcal{S}|\omega^{-s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}^{\dagger}|\mathcal{S}^{\perp}\rangle]

\displaystyle\geq\theta^{2}+(1-\theta)^{2}-2\theta(1-\theta)

(
\operatorname{Re}[\langle\mathcal{S}|\omega^{-s(\mathbf{x})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}^{\dagger}|\mathcal{S}^{\perp}\rangle]\in[-1,1]
)

\displaystyle=(2\theta-1)^{2}.\qed

3
Bell sampling on qudits

In this section, we generalise Bell sampling as proposed by
[
Mon17
,
GNW21
]
to qudits. To do so, we first introduce the generalised Bell states. Let
|\Phi^{+}\rangle\triangleq d^{-n/2}\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n}}|\mathbf{q}\rangle^{\otimes 2}
be a maximally entangled state. The generalised Bell states over
\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n}
are defined as

\displaystyle|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\rangle\triangleq(\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\otimes\mathbf{I})|\Phi^{+}\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d^{n}}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n}}\tau^{\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}\rangle}\cdot\omega^{\langle\mathbf{q},\mathbf{v}\rangle}|\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{w}\rangle|\mathbf{q}\rangle,\quad\forall\mathbf{x}=(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}.

Notice that
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\rangle=d^{-\frac{n}{2}}\operatorname{vec}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}})
since
\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}=\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n}}\tau^{\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}\rangle}\cdot\omega^{\langle\mathbf{q},\mathbf{v}\rangle}|\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{w}\rangle\langle\mathbf{q}|
. As previously mentioned, the re-scaled Weyl operators
\{d^{-\frac{n}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}:\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}\}
form an orthonormal basis in the space of operators, therefore the states
\{|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\rangle:\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}\}
form an orthonormal basis in the Hilbert space
(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}\otimes(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
. Indeed,
\langle\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}\rangle=d^{-n}\operatorname{Tr}[\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}^{\dagger}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}]=\mathbf{1}[\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{y}]
. In addition, note that, for all
\mathbf{x}=(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}),\mathbf{y}=(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{w}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
,

\displaystyle(\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}})|\Phi^{+}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d^{n}}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n}}\tau^{\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{w}^{\prime}\rangle}\cdot\omega^{\langle\mathbf{q},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle}|\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{w}\rangle|\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{w}^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d^{n}}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n}}\tau^{\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{w}^{\prime}\rangle}\cdot\omega^{\langle\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{w}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle}|\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{w}-\mathbf{w}^{\prime}\rangle|\mathbf{q}\rangle

\displaystyle=\tau^{\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}\rangle-\langle\mathbf{v^{\prime}},\mathbf{w^{\prime}}\rangle-2\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w^{\prime}}\rangle}\cdot\tau^{-\langle\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{w}-\mathbf{w}^{\prime}\rangle}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}-J(\mathbf{y})}\otimes\mathbf{I})|\Phi^{+}\rangle

\displaystyle=\tau^{[J(\mathbf{y}),\mathbf{x}]}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}-J(\mathbf{y})}\otimes\mathbf{I})|\Phi^{+}\rangle.

(3)

Thus
(\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{J(\mathbf{x})})|\Phi^{+}\rangle=|\Phi^{+}\rangle
. The following representation of
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\rangle\langle\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|
will be useful.

Lemma 36
.

For all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\rangle\langle\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|=\frac{1}{d^{2n}}\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}\omega^{[\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{J(\mathbf{y})}.

Proof.

For all
\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}\omega^{[\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}]}\langle\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}}|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{J(\mathbf{y})}|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}^{\prime}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}\omega^{[\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}]}\langle\Phi^{+}|(\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}\otimes\mathbb{I})(\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{J(\mathbf{y})})(\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbb{I})|\Phi^{+}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}\omega^{[\mathbf{y},\mathbf{z}^{\prime}-\mathbf{x}]}\langle\Phi^{+}|(\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbb{I})(\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{J(\mathbf{y})})|\Phi^{+}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}\omega^{[\mathbf{y},\mathbf{z}^{\prime}-\mathbf{x}]}\langle\Phi^{+}|(\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbb{I})|\Phi^{+}\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}[\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{z}^{\prime}]\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}\omega^{[\mathbf{y},\mathbf{z}^{\prime}-\mathbf{x}]}

\displaystyle=d^{2n}\mathbf{1}[\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{z}^{\prime}=\mathbf{x}],

using
Lemma

9
together with
(\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n})^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}=\{\mathbf{0}\}
(the symplectic product is non-degenerate).
∎

Given a pure state of
2n
qudits divided into systems
A_{1},\dots,A_{n}
and
B_{1},\dots,B_{n}
, we call
Bell sampling
the operation of measuring each pair
A_{i}B_{i}
of qudits in the generalised Bell basis, which returns a vector in
\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
. A similar operation, called
Bell difference sampling
, performs Bell sampling twice and subtracts the results from each other.

Definition 37

(Bell sampling)
.

Bell sampling is the projective measurement given by
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\rangle\langle\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|
for
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
.

Definition 38

(Bell difference sampling)
.

Bell difference sampling is defined as performing Bell sampling twice and subtracting the results from each other (modulo
d
). More precisely, it is the projective measurement given by

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbf{x}}=\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}\rangle\langle\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}|\otimes|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}}\rangle\langle\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}}|,\quad\forall\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}.

Performing Bell sampling on
|\psi_{1}\rangle|\psi_{2}\rangle
is equivalent to sampling from a surprisingly simple probability distribution.

Lemma 39
.

Given two pure states
|\psi_{1}\rangle,|\psi_{2}\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
, Bell sampling on
|\psi_{1}\rangle|\psi_{2}\rangle
returns
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
with probability
d^{-n}|\langle\psi_{1}|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\psi_{2}^{\ast}\rangle|^{2}
. In particular, if
|\psi_{1}\rangle=|\psi_{2}^{\ast}\rangle=|\psi\rangle
, Bell sampling on
|\psi\rangle|\psi^{\ast}\rangle
corresponds to sampling from the distribution
p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})
.

Proof.

We have
|\langle\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|(|\psi_{1}\rangle|\psi_{2}\rangle)|^{2}=d^{-n}|{\operatorname{Tr}}[\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}^{\dagger}|\psi_{1}\rangle\langle\psi_{2}^{\ast}|]|^{2}=d^{-n}|\langle\psi_{1}|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}|\psi_{2}^{\ast}\rangle|^{2}
, since
|\psi_{1}\rangle|\psi_{2}\rangle=\operatorname{vec}(|\psi_{1}\rangle\langle\psi_{2}^{\ast}|)
.
∎

As an immediate consequence of the above result, performing Bell sampling on
|\mathcal{S}\rangle|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle
is equivalent to sampling from
p_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{x})
, which according to
Equation

2
is constant on
\mathscr{M}
. This means that Bell sampling on
|\mathcal{S}\rangle|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle
returns an element of
\mathscr{M}
.

It is well known that the transformation
|\psi\rangle\mapsto|\psi^{\ast}\rangle
cannot be implemented by a physical process since it is not completely positive. Therefore, there is little hope to sample from
p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})
using Bell sampling with only access to copies of
|\psi\rangle
. The situation is more subtle if we restrict ourselves to stabiliser states
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
. First observe that
|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle
is itself a stabiliser state since
\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}}^{\ast}=(-1)^{(d+1)\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}\rangle}\mathcal{W}_{J(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})}
. This means that if
|\mathcal{S}\rangle=|\mathscr{M},s\rangle
, then
|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle=|J(\mathscr{M}),t\rangle
, where
\omega^{t(\mathbf{x})}=\omega^{-s(\mathbf{x})}(-1)^{(d+1)\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}\rangle}
.

For qubits (
d=2
), the involution operation
J
is trivial, i.e.,
J(\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{x}
for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{2n}
. As a consequence, the stabiliser state
|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle
is characterised by the same Lagrangian subspace as
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
, possibly only with a different phase. Therefore,
|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle=\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}}|\mathcal{S}\rangle
for some
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{2n}
, i.e.,
|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle
is related to
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
via a Weyl operator
\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}}
, which ultimately is the reason why a simple Bell sampling on
|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 2}
works. Allcock et al.
[
ADIS24
]
explored Bell (difference) sampling on
|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 4}
for any
2
2

2

Allcock et al.
[
ADIS24
]
technically focused on prime dimensions, but their results can be generalised to any
d
.
dimension
d
and found that it returns a random string
\mathbf{x}
in
\mathscr{M}+J(\mathscr{M})
which, in the worst case, is the whole space
\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
. A new idea is thus needed in order to sample elements from
\mathscr{M}
.

We now show how one can uniformly sample from the Lagrangian submodule
\mathscr{M}
. At the core of our sampling procedure, we develop an algorithm that maps copies of a (possibly unknown) stabiliser state
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
into its complex conjugate
|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle
. More precisely, we show that there is a unitary
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}\in((\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n})^{\otimes 4}
that transforms four copies of
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
into four copies of
|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle
up to some Weyl operator and phase depending on
\mathcal{S}
. Interestingly enough, the unitary
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}
is constructed using Lagrange’s four-square theorem, which states that every positive integer can be written as the sum of four integer squares (hence why we need four copies of
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
). In the following, we denote a dependence of
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n}
and
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}
on some stabiliser group
\mathcal{S}
as
\mathbf{v}(\mathcal{S})
and
s(\mathcal{S})
.

Definition 40
.

For integers
d\geq 2
,
k\geq 1
, and matrix
\mathbf{R}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{k\times k}
, let the unitary
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}\in((\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n})^{\otimes k}

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}:|\mathbf{Q}\rangle\mapsto|\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}~(\operatorname{mod}d)\rangle,\quad\text{where}\quad\mathbf{Q}=[\mathbf{q}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{q}_{k}]\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n\times k}.

Fact 41

(Lagrange’s four-square theorem)
.

For every
m\in\mathbb{N}
, there are
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
such that
m=a_{1}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}+a_{3}^{2}+a_{4}^{2}
.

Lemma 42
.

For integer
d\geq 2
, let
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
such that
a_{1}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}+a_{3}^{2}+a_{4}^{2}=D-1
. Then the matrix

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}&a_{2}&a_{3}&a_{4}\\
a_{2}&-a_{1}&a_{4}&-a_{3}\\
a_{3}&-a_{4}&-a_{1}&a_{2}\\
a_{4}&a_{3}&-a_{2}&-a_{1}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4\times 4}

satisfies
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\top}=(D-1)\mathbf{I}
.

Theorem 43
.

For every integer
d\geq 2
, consider the unitary
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}\in((\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n})^{\otimes 4}
from
Definition

40
with any
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4\times 4}
such that
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\top}=(D-1)\mathbf{I}
. Then for any stabiliser group
\mathcal{S}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 4}=\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{w}_{i}(\mathcal{S})}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{v}_{i}(\mathcal{S}),\mathbf{0}}\right)\omega^{s(\mathcal{S)}}|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle^{\otimes 4}

for some
\mathbf{v}_{1}(\mathcal{S}),\dots,\mathbf{v}_{4}(\mathcal{S}),\mathbf{w}_{1}(\mathcal{S}),\dots,\mathbf{w}_{4}(\mathcal{S})\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n}
and
s(\mathcal{S})\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}
depending on
\mathcal{S}
.

Proof.

According to
Lemma

23
, every stabiliser state
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
can be written as (up to normalisation)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{S}\rangle=\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell}}\omega^{\mathbf{p}^{\top}\mathbf{q}}\cdot\tau^{\mathbf{q}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{q}}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{u}\rangle,\quad\text{where}~\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{u}~\text{and}~\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{W}.

Write
\mathbf{q}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{q}=\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{q}\mathbf{q}^{\top}]
and
\mathbf{p}^{\top}\mathbf{q}=\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{q}\mathbf{p}^{\top}]
. Then

\displaystyle|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 4}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{q}_{1},\mathbf{q}_{2},\mathbf{q}_{3},\mathbf{q}_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell}}\tau^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{M}\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathbf{q}_{i}\mathbf{q}_{i}^{\top}]}\cdot\omega^{\operatorname{Tr}[\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathbf{q}_{i}\mathbf{p}^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}_{1}+\mathbf{u},\cdots,\mathbf{W}\mathbf{q}_{4}+\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell\times 4}}\tau^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{Q}^{\top}]}\cdot\omega^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}\rangle,

where
\mathbf{Q}=[\mathbf{q}_{1},\mathbf{q}_{2},\mathbf{q}_{3},\mathbf{q}_{4}]\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell\times 4}
is a matrix with columns
\mathbf{q}_{1},\mathbf{q}_{2},\mathbf{q}_{3},\mathbf{q}_{4}
, and similarly for
\mathbf{U}=[\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}]\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell\times 4}
and
\mathbf{P}=[\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}]\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell\times 4}
.

Consider now the unitary
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}:|\mathbf{Q}\rangle\mapsto|\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}~(\operatorname{mod}d)\rangle
from
Definition

40
, where
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4\times 4}
is such that
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\top}=(D-1)\mathbf{I}
.
Notice that
\mathbf{Y}\equiv\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}~(\operatorname{mod}d)\implies\mathbf{Y}\equiv\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}+\mathbf{E}~(\operatorname{mod}D)
for some
\mathbf{E}\in\mathbb{Z}_{D}^{\ell\times 4}
such that
\mathbf{E}\equiv\mathbf{0}~(\operatorname{mod}d)
and
2\mathbf{E}\equiv\mathbf{0}~(\operatorname{mod}D)
. We can see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 4}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell\times 4}}\tau^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{Q}^{\top}]}\cdot\omega^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}+\mathbf{U}\mathbf{R}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell\times 4}}\tau^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{M}(-\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}(\operatorname{mod}d))(-\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}(\operatorname{mod}d))^{\top}]}\cdot\omega^{-\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{P}^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}\mathbf{R}\rangle

(by
\mathbf{Q}\leftarrow-\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}(\operatorname{mod}d)
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell\times 4}}\tau^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top})(\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top})^{\top}]}\cdot\omega^{-\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{P}^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}\mathbf{R}\rangle

(for some
\mathbf{E}\in\mathbb{Z}_{D}^{\ell\times 4}
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell\times 4}}\tau^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Q}^{\top}-\mathbf{M}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Q}^{\top}-\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{\top}+\mathbf{M}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\top}]}\cdot\omega^{-\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{P}^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}\mathbf{R}\rangle.

Notice now that, if
d
is odd, then
2\mathbf{E}\equiv\mathbf{0}~(\operatorname{mod}D)\implies\mathbf{E}\equiv\mathbf{0}~(\operatorname{mod}2d)
. If
d
is even, then
\mathbf{E}\equiv\mathbf{0}~(\operatorname{mod}d)\implies\mathbf{E}^{\top}\equiv\mathbf{0}~(\operatorname{mod}2)
, i.e., all the entries of
\mathbf{E}^{\top}
are even, and so
\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\equiv 2\mathbf{E}\mathbf{I}_{\ell\times 4}\equiv\mathbf{0}~(\operatorname{mod}2d)
. In both cases,
\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\equiv\mathbf{0}~(\operatorname{mod}D)
. Regarding the term
\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Q}^{\top}+\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{\top}]=\operatorname{Tr}[(\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{M}^{\top})\mathbf{E}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Q}^{\top}]
(using the trace cyclicality), we know that
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{M}^{\top}
is symmetrical, therefore
\operatorname{Tr}[(\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{M}^{\top})\mathbf{E}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Q}^{\top}]=\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{M}(2\mathbf{E})\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Q}^{\top}]=0
, since
2\mathbf{E}\equiv\mathbf{0}~(\operatorname{mod}D)
. Hence, the expression
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 4}
simplifies to

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 4}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell\times 4}}\tau^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Q}^{\top}]}\cdot\omega^{-\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{P}^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}\mathbf{R}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell\times 4}}\tau^{-\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{Q}^{\top}]}\cdot\omega^{-\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{P}^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}\mathbf{R}\rangle.

(since
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}=(D-1)\mathbf{I}
)

The above expression is almost
|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle^{\otimes 4}
, the only issue being the remaining matrices
\mathbf{R}
. It thus only remains to show that the above expression can be transformed into
|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle^{\otimes 4}
via some Weyl operator. For this, we use that, according to
Lemma

23
,
\mathbf{p}\in\operatorname{row}(\mathbf{W})\implies\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{t}
for some
\mathbf{t}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n}
. Let
\mathbf{T}=[\mathbf{t},\mathbf{t},\mathbf{t},\mathbf{t}]\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{\ell\times 4}
. In the following, let
(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{I})_{i}
be the
i
-th column of
\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{I}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{4\times 4}
. We note that, by the definition of the Weyl operator
\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}}
,

\displaystyle\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{U}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{I})_{i}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{T}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{R})_{i},\mathbf{0}}\right)|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}\rangle

\displaystyle=\omega^{\sum_{i=1}^{4}(\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}_{i}+\mathbf{U}_{i})^{\top}(\mathbf{T}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{R})_{i})}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}+\mathbf{U}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{I})\rangle

\displaystyle=\omega^{\operatorname{Tr}[(\mathbf{T}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{R})(\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U})^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}\mathbf{R}\rangle

\displaystyle=\omega^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{T}\mathbf{Q}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{\top}-\mathbf{T}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Q}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{R})\mathbf{U}^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}\mathbf{R}\rangle

\displaystyle=\omega^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}^{\top}-\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{P}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{R})\mathbf{U}^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}\mathbf{R}\rangle.

In summary,

\displaystyle\omega^{-\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{P}^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}\mathbf{R}\rangle=\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{U}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{I})_{i}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{T}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{R})_{i},\mathbf{0}}\right)\omega^{\operatorname{Tr}[-\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{I})\mathbf{U}^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}\rangle,

which means that

\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 4}=\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{U}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{I})_{i}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{T}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{R})_{i},\mathbf{0}}\right)\omega^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{T}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{I})\mathbf{U}^{\top}]}|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle^{\otimes 4}.\qed

Remark 44
.

Since
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\dagger}=\mathcal{B}_{-\mathbf{R}^{\top}}
, then also
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\dagger}|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 4}=\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle^{\otimes 4}
for some Pauli
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})
depending on
\mathcal{S}
.

From the above proof, it should be clear that the number of copies of
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
required by the unitary
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}
is bounded by the minimum number of integer squares required to decompose
D-1
. As a consequence, specific dimensions
d
might require less copies of
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
. As examples, the sum-of-two-squares theorem states that
m\in\mathbb{N}
can be written as the sum of two squares of integers,
m=a_{1}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}
, if and only if its prime decomposition contains no factor
p^{k}
, where prime
p\equiv 3~(\operatorname{mod}4)
and
k
is odd. More specifically for odd prime dimensions, it is well known that if
d\equiv 3~(\operatorname{mod}4)
, then there are
a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
such that
a_{1}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}\equiv-1~(\operatorname{mod}d)
. Also,
d\equiv 1~(\operatorname{mod}4)
if and only if
-1
is a quadratic residue modulo
d
, i.e., there is
a_{1}\in\mathbb{F}_{d}
such that
a_{1}^{2}\equiv-1~(\operatorname{mod}d)
. In this case, the unitary
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}
can be replaced by the identity. We summarise these remarks in the next result.

Corollary 45
.

Let
|\mathcal{S}\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
be any stabiliser state. Then:

1.

If
p^{k}\nmid(D-1)
for prime
p\equiv 3~(\operatorname{mod}4)
and
k
odd, then there is a unitary that maps
|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 2}\mapsto(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}_{i}})\tau^{s}|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle^{\otimes 2}
for all
|\mathcal{S}\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
, where
\mathbf{x}_{i}
and
s
depend on
\mathcal{S}
;

2.

If
d
is prime and
d\equiv 3~(\operatorname{mod}4)
, there is a unitary that maps
|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 2}\mapsto(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}_{i}})\omega^{s}|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle^{\otimes 2}
for all
|\mathcal{S}\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
, where
\mathbf{x}_{i}
and
s
depend on
\mathcal{S}
;

3.

If
d
is prime and
d\equiv 1~(\operatorname{mod}4)
, then
|\mathcal{S}\rangle=\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}}\omega^{s}|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle
where
\mathbf{x}
and
s
depend on
\mathcal{S}
.

As previously discussed, for qubits (
d=2
) both
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
and
|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle
are related by a Weyl operator, so there is no need to perform any transformation. If we employ
Theorems

43
and
45
for
d=2
, we paradoxically find that
3
copies of
|\mathcal{S}\rangle
would be required to obtain any copy of
|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle
(since
D-1=3
for
d=2
). However, since
\tau^{d}=\omega=-1
for
d=2
and the quadratic term
\mathbf{q}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{q}
becomes linear over
\mathbb{F}_{2}
, i.e.,
(-1)^{\mathbf{q}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{q}}=(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{ii}q_{i}}
due to
\mathbf{M}
being symmetric and
q_{i}^{2}=q_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}
, one only needs to decompose
d-1
, and not
D-1=2d-1
, into integer squares, which is trivial as
d-1=1
. Therefore, the procedure explained in
Theorem

43
also recovers the qubit case (
\mathcal{B}_{2}
is the identity).

Finally, we mention that there are randomised algorithms for computing a single representation
m=a_{1}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}+a_{3}^{2}+a_{4}^{2}
of a given
m\in\mathbb{N}
in expected time
O(\log^{2}{m}/\log\log{m})

[
RS86
,
PT18
]
.

Even though in
Theorem

43
we phrased the action of
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}
onto
|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 4}
, it is possible to describe the effect of
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}
onto tensor products of Weyl operators.

Theorem 46
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}\in((\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n})^{\otimes 4}
be the unitary from
Definition

40
with
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4\times 4}
such that
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\top}=(D-1)\mathbf{I}
.
Then, for all
\mathbf{X}=[\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{3},\mathbf{x}_{4}]\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n\times 4}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}_{i}}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\dagger}=\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\mathcal{W}_{J((\mathbf{X}\mathbf{R})_{i})},

where
(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{R})_{i}
is the
i
-th column of
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{R}
and
J:\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}\to\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
is the involution.

Proof.

Write
\mathbf{x}_{i}=(\mathbf{v}_{i},\mathbf{w}_{i})
for
i\in[4]
and let
\mathbf{V}=[\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{v}_{3},\mathbf{v}_{4}],\mathbf{W}=[\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{w}_{2},\mathbf{w}_{3},\mathbf{w}_{4}]\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n\times 4}
. For any
\mathbf{Q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{n\times 4}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}_{i}}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\dagger}|\mathbf{Q}\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}_{i}}\right)|-\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\rangle

\displaystyle=\tau^{\sum_{i=1}^{4}\langle\mathbf{v}_{i},\mathbf{w}_{i}\rangle}\cdot\omega^{-\sum_{i=1}^{4}\langle\mathbf{v}_{i},(\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top})_{i}\rangle}\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}|\mathbf{W}-\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\rangle

\displaystyle=\tau^{\sum_{i=1}^{4}\langle\mathbf{v}_{i},\mathbf{w}_{i}\rangle}\cdot\omega^{-\sum_{i=1}^{4}\langle\mathbf{v}_{i},(\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top})_{i}\rangle}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{R}+\mathbf{Q}\rangle

(
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}=(D-1)\mathbf{I}
)

\displaystyle=\tau^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{W}\mathbf{V}^{\top}]}\cdot\omega^{-\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{V}^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{R}+\mathbf{Q}\rangle

\displaystyle=\tau^{-\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{W}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{V}^{\top}]}\cdot\omega^{-\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{V}^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{R}+\mathbf{Q}\rangle

(
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}=(D-1)\mathbf{I}
)

\displaystyle=\tau^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{W}\mathbf{R}(-\mathbf{V}\mathbf{R})^{\top}]}\cdot\omega^{\operatorname{Tr}[\mathbf{Q}(-\mathbf{V}\mathbf{R})^{\top}]}|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{R}+\mathbf{Q}\rangle

\displaystyle=\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\mathcal{W}_{J((\mathbf{X}\mathbf{R})_{i})}|\mathbf{Q}\rangle.

∎

We define a modified Bell sampling procedure.

Definition 47

(
U
-skewed Bell sampling)
.

Let
U\in((\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n})^{\otimes 2k}
be a unitary. The
U
-skewed Bell sampling on
2k
states in
(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
is the projective measurement

\displaystyle U^{\dagger}\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[k]}|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}_{i}}\rangle\langle\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}_{i}}|\right)U\qquad\forall\mathbf{x}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{x}_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}.

Definition 48

(
U
-skewed Bell difference sampling)
.

Let
U\in((\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n})^{\otimes 4k}
be a unitary. The
U
-skewed Bell difference sampling on
4k
states in
(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
is the projective measurement

\displaystyle U^{\dagger}\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[k]}\Pi_{\mathbf{x}_{i}}\right)U\quad\text{where}\quad\Pi_{\mathbf{x}_{i}}=\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}\ |\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}\rangle\langle\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}|\otimes|\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}_{i}+\mathbf{y}}\rangle\langle\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}_{i}+\mathbf{y}}|,\quad\forall\mathbf{x}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{x}_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}.

Performing a skewed Bell sampling on copies of a stabiliser state
|\mathcal{S}\rangle=|\mathscr{M},s\rangle
returns elements from
\mathscr{M}+\mathbf{z}
for some
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
depending on
\mathcal{S}
.

Theorem 49
.

Let
|\mathcal{S}\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
be a stabiliser state. Let
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}\in((\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n})^{\otimes 4}
be the unitary from
Definition

40
with
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4\times 4}
such that
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\top}=(D-1)\mathbf{I}
.
Let
\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}\in((\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n})^{\otimes 8}
be the unitary acting on
\bigotimes_{i\in[8]}|\mathbf{q}_{i}\rangle
that applies
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}
onto
|\mathbf{q}_{2},\mathbf{q}_{4},\mathbf{q}_{6},\mathbf{q}_{8}\rangle
and the identity onto the remaining registers. Then
\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}
-skewed Bell sampling on
|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 8}
corresponds to sampling from the distribution
\prod_{i=1}^{4}p_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}+\mathbf{z}_{i}(\mathcal{S}))
for some
\mathbf{z}_{1}(\mathcal{S}),\dots,\mathbf{z}_{4}(\mathcal{S})\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
.

Proof.

The probability distribution is simply

\displaystyle\left|\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{4}\langle\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}_{i}}|\right)\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\dagger}|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 8}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{4}\langle\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}_{i}}|\right)\bigotimes_{i=1}^{4}\left((\mathbf{I}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}_{i}})|\mathcal{S}\rangle|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle\right)\right|^{2}

(by
Theorem

43
)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{4}|\langle\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{x}_{i}+J(\mathbf{z}_{i})}|(|\mathcal{S}\rangle|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle)|^{2}

(by
Equation

3
and
\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{y}}^{\dagger}=\mathcal{W}_{-\mathbf{y}}
)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{4}p_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}+J(\mathbf{z}_{i}))

(by
Lemma

39
)

for some
\mathbf{z}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{z}_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
that depend on
\mathcal{S}
. Simply relabel
\mathbf{z}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{z}_{4}
to
J(\mathbf{z}_{1}),\dots,J(\mathbf{z}_{4})
.
∎

We now explore skewed Bell difference sampling on
|\psi\rangle^{\otimes 16}
for any state
|\psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
and not only stabiliser states.

Theorem 50
.

Let
|\psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
be a pure state. Let
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}\in((\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n})^{\otimes 4}
be the unitary from
Definition

40
with
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4\times 4}
such that
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\top}=(D-1)\mathbf{I}
. Let
\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}\in((\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n})^{\otimes 8}
be the unitary acting on
\bigotimes_{i\in[8]}|\mathbf{q}_{i}\rangle
that applies
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{R}}
onto
|\mathbf{q}_{2},\mathbf{q}_{4},\mathbf{q}_{6},\mathbf{q}_{8}\rangle
and the identity onto the remaining registers. Then
(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}\otimes\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}})
-skewed Bell difference sampling on
|\psi\rangle^{\otimes 16}
corresponds to sampling from the distribution

\displaystyle b_{\psi}(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{3},\mathbf{x}_{4})

\displaystyle\triangleq\operatorname{Tr}\left[(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}\otimes\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}})\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\Pi_{\mathbf{x}_{i}}\right)(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\dagger}\otimes\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\dagger})\psi^{\otimes 16}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{Y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n\times 4}}\prod_{i=1}^{4}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x}_{i}+\mathbf{Y}_{i})p_{\psi}((\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{R})_{i}).

If
|\psi\rangle=|\mathcal{S}\rangle=|\mathscr{M},s\rangle
is a stabiliser state, then

\displaystyle b_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{3},\mathbf{x}_{4})=\begin{cases}d^{-4n}&\text{if}~\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{3},\mathbf{x}_{4}\in\mathscr{M},\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

We call the distribution
b_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})
the
\mathbf{R}
-skewed (or simply skewed) Bell distribution of
|\psi\rangle
.

Proof.

First note that

\displaystyle d^{16n}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}\otimes\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}})\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\Pi_{\mathbf{x}_{i}}\right)(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\dagger}\otimes\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{y}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{y}_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}\!\!\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}\!\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\sum_{\mathbf{z}^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}\omega^{[\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{z}_{i}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}^{\prime}_{i}}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{J(\mathbf{z}^{\prime}_{i})}\!\!\right)\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\dagger}\otimes\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}\!\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\sum_{\mathbf{z}_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}\omega^{[\mathbf{x}_{i}+\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{z}_{i}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}^{\prime}_{i}}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{J(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\!\!\right)\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\dagger}

(by
Lemma

36
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{y}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{y}_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}\sum_{\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n\times 4}}\omega^{\sum_{i=1}^{4}[\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}_{i}+\mathbf{Z}_{i}]}\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}_{i}}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{(\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}\mathbf{R})_{i}}\right)\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\omega^{[\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{Z}_{i}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{Z}_{i}}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{(\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{R})_{i}}\right)

(by
Theorem

46
)

\displaystyle=d^{8n}\sum_{\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n\times 4}}\mathbf{1}[\mathbf{Z}=-\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}]\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}_{i}}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{(\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}\mathbf{R})_{i}}\right)\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\omega^{[\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{Z}_{i}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{Z}_{i}}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{(\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{R})_{i}}\right)

(by
Lemma

9
)

\displaystyle=d^{8n}\sum_{\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n\times 4}}\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{Z}_{i}}^{\dagger}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{(\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{R})_{i}}^{\dagger}\right)\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\omega^{[\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{Z}_{i}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{Z}_{i}}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{(\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{R})_{i}}\right).

(due to
\mathcal{W}_{-\mathbf{z}}=\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}
)

Therefore,

\displaystyle b_{\psi}(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{3},\mathbf{x}_{4})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d^{8n}}\sum_{\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n\times 4}}\!\operatorname{Tr}\!\left[\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{Z}_{i}}^{\dagger}\psi\otimes\mathcal{W}_{(\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{R})_{i}}^{\dagger}\psi\right)\!\otimes\!\left(\bigotimes_{i\in[4]}\omega^{[\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{Z}_{i}]}\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{Z}_{i}}\psi\otimes\mathcal{W}_{(\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{R})_{i}}\psi\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n\times 4}}\prod_{i=1}^{4}\omega^{[\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{Z}_{i}]}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{Z}_{i})p_{\psi}((\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{R})_{i}).

(4)

The above expression can be further simplified to

\displaystyle b_{\psi}(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{3},\mathbf{x}_{4})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n\times 4}}\prod_{i=1}^{4}\omega^{[\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{Z}_{i}]}\omega^{[\mathbf{Z}_{i},\mathbf{Y}_{i}]+[(\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{R})_{i},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{i}]}\widehat{p_{\psi}}(\mathbf{Y}_{i})\widehat{p_{\psi}}(\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{i})

(Fourier expansion)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d^{8n}}\sum_{\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n\times 4}}\prod_{i=1}^{4}\omega^{[\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{Z}_{i}]}\omega^{[\mathbf{Z}_{i},\mathbf{Y}_{i}]+[(\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{R})_{i},(\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}\mathbf{R})_{i}]}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{Y}_{i})p_{\psi}((\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}\mathbf{R})_{i})

(by
Lemma

15
and
\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}\mathbf{R}
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d^{8n}}\sum_{\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n\times 4}}\prod_{i=1}^{4}\omega^{[\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{Z}_{i}]}\omega^{[\mathbf{Z}_{i},\mathbf{Y}_{i}]-[\mathbf{Z}_{i},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{i}]}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{Y}_{i})p_{\psi}((\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}\mathbf{R})_{i})

(due to
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}=(D-1)\mathbf{I}
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{Y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n\times 4}}\prod_{i=1}^{4}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x}_{i}+\mathbf{Y}_{i})p_{\psi}((\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{R})_{i}).

(by
Lemma

9
)

If
|\psi\rangle=|\mathcal{S}\rangle=|\mathscr{M},s\rangle
is a stabiliser state, then we know that
p_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{y})=d^{-n}
if
\mathbf{y}\in\mathscr{M}
and
p_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{y})=0
if
\mathbf{y}\notin\mathscr{M}
. This means that
\prod_{i=1}^{4}p_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}+\mathbf{Y}_{i})p_{\mathcal{S}}((\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{R})_{i})\neq 0
if and only if
\mathbf{x}_{i}+\mathbf{Y}_{i}\in\mathscr{M}
and
(\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{R})_{i}\in\mathscr{M}
for
i\in[4]
. Since
\mathscr{M}
is a submodule, this implies that
\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{Y}_{i}\in\mathscr{M}
for
i\in[4]
if and only if
\prod_{i=1}^{4}p_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}+\mathbf{Y}_{i})p_{\mathcal{S}}((\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{R})_{i})\neq 0
. Therefore,

b_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{3},\mathbf{x}_{4})=\begin{cases}d^{-4n}&\text{if}~\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{3},\mathbf{x}_{4}\in\mathscr{M},\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}\qed

Theorem

50
is the much sought-after generalisation of Bell sampling: a properly skewed Bell difference sampling can successfully sample generators from a stabiliser group if sufficient copies of its stabiliser state are given. We compare the above result with the qubit case (
d=2
)
b_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})=2^{2n}(p_{\psi}\ast p_{\psi})(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{2n}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y})p_{\psi}(\mathbf{y})
from Gross, Nezami, and Walter
[
GNW21
]
. Due to the action of the matrix
\mathbf{R}
in order to map
|\mathcal{S}\rangle^{\otimes 4}\mapsto|\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\rangle^{\otimes 4}
, all characteristic distributions inside
b_{\psi}
become non-trivially correlated. From now on, whenever we mention the matrix
\mathbf{R}
, we refer to the
4\times 4
integer matrix from
Lemma

42
. Finally, we shall succinctly write
b_{\psi}(\mathbf{X})
for
\mathbf{X}=[\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{3},\mathbf{x}_{4}]\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n\times 4}
.

3.1
Further properties of distributions
p_{\psi}
and
b_{\psi}

The distributions
p_{\psi}
and
b_{\psi}
are quite rich and we now explore them in more details. From
Equations

4
and
9
, we obtain the following simple corollaries. The first regards the mass on a submodule
\mathscr{X}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
under
b_{\psi}
.

Corollary 51
.

Let
\mathscr{X}_{1},\mathscr{X}_{2},\mathscr{X}_{3},\mathscr{X}_{4}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
be submodules and
\mathbf{X}=[\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{3},\mathbf{x}_{4}]\in\bigotimes_{i=1}^{4}\mathscr{X}_{i}
be the matrix with columns
\mathbf{x}_{i}\in\mathscr{X}_{i}
,
i\in[4]
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\prod_{i=1}^{4}|\mathscr{X}_{i}|}\sum_{\mathbf{X}\in\bigotimes_{i=1}^{4}\mathscr{X}_{i}}b_{\psi}(\mathbf{X})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{X}\in\bigotimes_{i=1}^{4}\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}}}\prod_{i=1}^{4}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{X}_{i})p_{\psi}((\mathbf{X}\mathbf{R})_{i}).

The second gives an expression for the marginal distribution of
b_{\psi}
.

Corollary 52
.

For all
|\psi\rangle\in(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{3},\mathbf{x}_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}b_{\psi}(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{3},\mathbf{x}_{4})=d^{3n}\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}}\omega^{[\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{y}]}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{y})\prod_{i=1}^{4}p_{\psi}(a_{i}\mathbf{y}).

Corollary

51
has several important applications. The first one tells us that the mass of
b_{\psi}
on a submodule can be upper bounded by the
p_{\psi}
-mass.

Lemma 53
.

For any submodule
\mathscr{X}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2n}
,
\sum_{\mathbf{X}\in\mathscr{X}^{4}}b_{\psi}(\mathbf{X})\leq\big(\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{X}}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{x})\big)^{4}
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{X}\in\mathscr{X}^{4}}b_{\psi}(\mathbf{X})

\displaystyle=|\mathscr{X}|^{4}\sum_{\mathbf{X}\in(\mathscr{X}^{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}})^{4}}\prod_{i=1}^{4}p_{\psi}(\mathbf{X}_{i})p_{\psi}((\mathbf{X}\mathbf{R})_{i})

(by
Corollary

51
)
